ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43228	President of the Croatian Parliament, honourable MPs, respected MPs. The Government of the Republic of Croatia has therefore proposed the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on judicial cooperation in criminal matters with the Member States of the European Union. This law therefore addresses the issue of judicial cooperation in criminal matters between EU Member States. This law actually transposes the EU's legal rules into the national legal order. The law was adopted on 9 July 2010 with effect from 1.7.2013. In fact, these laws regulate the forms of judicial cooperation such as the European Arrest Warrant, such as the recognition of the execution of orders for the confiscation of assets, such as the recognition of the execution of decisions on fines, judgments and decisions imposing a sentence of imprisonment or a measure of confiscation of freedom as well as judgments and decisions laying down trial measures and alternative sanctions, and which recognise and enforce a decision on precautionary measures and a European protection order. These are the 4th amendments and additions to the law. The purpose of these amendments is to align ourselves with the newly created acquis in this area and to remove some observed weaknesses resulting from the application itself in practice. It is a matter of harmonisation with two directives, of the Directive on the right to access to a lawyer and of the Directive on the European Criminal Investigation Order. As far as this first directive is concerned, it is therefore the right to access a lawyer in criminal proceedings. They therefore establish minimum standards governing the procedural rights of the defendant in criminal proceedings. What is important is that this directive has also requested amendments and additions to the Law on criminal proceedings currently contained in the second reading, so it is necessary to watch them together. As regards the Directive on the European Research Order, this Directive intends, in fact, in a comprehensive way to regulate the implementation and provision of evidence in all cross-border cases that have been actually arranged in a fragmented way so far. In fact, this directive replaces the early EU rules governing this area. As I said, therefore, with this law, amendments and additions, we have actually tried to implement certain technical coordinations that the judicial authorities have seen through practice as weaknesses for therefore, normal functioning of EU rules. The law is scheduled to enter into force 8 days after its publication in the National Journal. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43234	Thank you, well-respected President of the Croatian Parliament, State Secretary with associates, respected colleagues and fellow MPs. What is really important is that the implementation of Directive 2013/48 also required the amendment of the Criminal Procedure Act, as said by counterpart Orsat, which will also come to us on the benches, and for the reason that the provisions governing the right of suspects and accused persons in the domestic criminal proceedings are in conformity with the procedures under the European arrest warrant. It is also important to note that the amended articles of the laws governing the rights of the requested person are in place in the execution of the European arrest warrant. A new article relating to the rights of the requested persons in relation to which a European arrest warrant has been issued has been introduced. What is also already pointed out and very important is the setting up of the Institute of Defence in the procedures of issuing, executing a European arrest warrant, and therefore in the law and the new article that is prescribed in the case where the person for whom a European arrest warrant has been issued in the Republic of Croatia after the arrest in another Member State wishes to exercise his right to defend himself in the Republic of Croatia. It is further prescribed that if the requested person wishes to exercise the right to defend himself in the issuing State, the State lawyer should inform the competent authority of the issuing State. It is also prescribed to restrict the right of the person sought to defend, i.e. to inform and communicate with third parties in accordance with domestic law if requested by the State of issuing a European arrest warrant. Mandatory defence attorneys have been prescribed at the hearing for the purpose of handing over to the judge of the investigation, which is also important for the protection of the suspect's procedural rights. As regards the implementation of Directive 2014/41, it should be pointed out that a new form of judicial cooperation has been introduced through a European investigation warrant. This order shall be issued by a Member State carrying out criminal or criminal proceedings and, for the purpose of carrying out one or more evidence actions in a country carrying out a European investigation warrant to collect evidence. Therefore, a new Chapter IIa was introduced into the law. which arranges those institutes. In addition, Chapter IV has been deleted for the purpose of implementing those Directives and the provisions of Chapters I and III have been amended. The law. Provisions on the competence of the issuing, receiving and forwarding of certain forms of judicial cooperation and provisions on costs and remedies have also been amended and in order to comply with the latest amendments to the law. The law also sets out a new article that sets out the principle of protection of fundamental rights as one of the fundamental principles of judicial cooperation in EU Member States, and as regards the suspension of the procedure, the situation is regulated when, after the power of a solution approving surrender, it is established that the reasons for refusing to carry out a European investigative order or suspending the procedure for the execution of a European investigative order, since the existing legal gap has created problems so far in practice. A provision that regulates the postponement of surrender and temporary surrender has also been amended, as the provisions so far have not been finalised in respect of the proceedings which also led to unequal case-law. A provision that regulates the situation when an investigative prison is abolished for surrender due to the expiry of the pre-inclusive deadlines for the execution of the surrender has also been amended. So this law sets out in fact minimum standards that strengthen mutual trust and facilitate judicial cooperation based on the principle of mutual recognition which is very positive and leads in fact to greater legal certainty, especially since it is a criminal law area where things are actually very sensitive because there is a restriction on freedom as a fundamental human right. These will therefore support the HDZ Club's proposal. It is also important to point out that for the implementation of this law, it will not be necessary from the current analyses is visible that there will be no need to provide additional funds in the Republic of Croatia's budget. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43238	Dear President of the Croatian Parliament, honourable MPs and MPs. The government of the Republic of Croatia proposes the Law on International Private Law. This area is currently governed by the law that the Law on conflicting laws with the regulations of other countries in certain relations, which was adopted in 1982 and which was taken over in our legal order. It's been 35 years, there have been significant and numerous changes. The existing legal solutions in the so-called The ZRS, short as we call it, are mostly obsolete and needs to be modernised. The law on international private law, therefore, would regulate three groups of rules. Rules on the determination of the applicable law for private legal relations with international character, rules on jurisdiction of courts and other bodies of the Republic of Croatia, for debate in legal matters subject to private legal relations with international character, and rules for the recognition and declaration of enforcement of foreign court decisions, the subject of which is the subject of private legal relations. By this law, we introduce a certain newspaper and one of the newspapers is the introduction of a link between the usual residence used in European law, but which is not defined, so we define it in law, but only for the purposes of applying this law, in order to facilitate the application of this law in practice. For the purpose of transparency of the relevant regulations of a specific provision of the Law on International Private Law, they refer to relevant EU regulations and international agreements. In this respect, the law follows the approach they adopt in other Member States, in which more recently there has been the adoption of such a modified regulation. So, for example. in Belgium, the Netherlands, the Czech Republic, Bulgaria, Romania. Also, this law sought to avoid dualism, to have a dualist system, or to have a difference in the approach to the application of the regulations of relevant law, the rules of jurisdiction relating to EU Member States and third countries. In this context, we have then used identical links, like those provided for in the regulations of European rights, and then we have extended them to relations with third countries. What is important to note is that this law has a subsidiary application, which means that it applies only after international contracts and only after European rules. So, when those regulations don't regulate anything. As it is a very demanding and complex law, we have foreseen one longer... to prepare the judiciary for the proper application of this law through the necessary training. It is important to point out that the adoption of this law and judges or practitioners will significantly facilitate the handling and resolution of situations with an international character because they will have a new law that will take over all the good case-law that has been put forward for years in solving all these disputes that have had international characteristics, and we will also get a law that meets the needs of modern international legal traffic that will lead to legal certainty and even more uniform jurisprudence. Here is why we welcome the adoption of this law as a club and we believe that it will indeed contribute to our whole legal order to a better and more uniform court practice. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43252	The esteemed President of the Croatian Parliament, MPs. The draft law on the regulation of the rail market and the protection of passenger rights in rail transport regulates the area of regulation of the rail market and the area of protection of passenger rights in rail transport, the competence and authority of the national regulatory authority and inspection. The existing Law on the regulation of the 2014 rail market needs to be amended in order to further comply with the EU acquis. In particular, regarding the regulations of the 4th railway package relating to the full opening of the market for passenger domestic rail transport services from 1 January 2019 and the tasks and responsibilities of the national regulatory authority. In 2016, the European Commission, as part of the EU pilot, submitted a question to the Republic of Croatia concerning the disagreement of national measures with the 2012 Directive. 34 EU. It is therefore necessary to amend the relevant provisions of the existing law. The proposal for a law lays down that the national regulatory authority is competent to carry out regulatory and other operations in the area of the rail market. Croatian Regulatory Agency for Network Activities, HACOM. The draft law relating to the regulatory body contains provisions on the principles of work on the duties of members of the Council, on decision-making of jobs, on decision-making and enforcement of decisions of judicial protection, on means of carrying out tasks and on the submission of a work report. The draft law concerning the regulation of the rail market contains provisions on regulatory principles and objectives of the regulatory body, on the jurisdiction of the regulatory authority, on the procedure under the request, on the collection of data, on the verification of accounting separation, on the determination of the basic purpose of the service of international rail transport of passengers including cabotage applicable only from 1 January 2019 on the determination of the economic balance of the public transport contract, on the issue of prior consent and non-binding opinions and assessments, on the setting of a time limit for access to the service facility, on the monitoring of the compensation for rail services, and on cooperation with national authorities and regulatory bodies of other EU Member States. The proposal for a law lays down that the regulatory authority is also responsible for the protection of the rights of passengers in rail transport, the proposal contains provisions on the jurisdiction of the regulatory authority for the implementation of the Regulation of the European Community No. 1371/2007 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 23 October 2007 on the rights to obligations of passengers in rail transport, on official duty in the protection of passenger rights, on the settlement in the procedure required by passengers in relation to the advertising of the Consumer Advertising Committee on rail transport and on inspection of passenger rights. Proposal for a law relating to the inspection supervision of the regulatory authority of the implementation of the law in question of the Regulation of the European Community No. Regulation (EU) No 1311/2007 and other rules governing the rights of passengers determines the conduct of inspection tasks, the powers of inspectors and the obligations of supervised and other persons participating in inspection surveillance. The implementation of this law does not require the provision of additional financial resources in the state budget. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43274	... President-in-Office of the Parliament, distinguished representatives of the motions from the Ministry of the Sea, Transport and Infrastructure, distinguished colleagues and colleagues. [[The speaker is not enabled.]] As the Proposer has already said, this proposal lays down the area of regulation of the rail market, the area of protection of passenger rights in rail transport, and the jurisdiction and authority of the national regulatory authority, including the tasks of inspection supervision of the implementation of this law. We know that these issues have already been identified by the existing law of 2014 but with a view to further harmonising with the EU acquis, it needs to be replaced. As part of the fourth railway package for the opening of the market for passenger domestic rail transport and rail infrastructure management services, EU Directive 2016/2370 of the European Parliament entered into force at the end of December 2016, and this Directive substantially amended the provisions of Directive 2012/34 of the EU, in relation to the business and competence of the national regulatory authority. This draft law provides that the national regulatory authority is competent to carry out regulatory and other operations in the field of rail services markets, the Croatian Regulatory Agency for Network Activities, HACOM. The proposal for a law lays down that the regulatory authority is also responsible for the protection of passenger rights in rail transport as well as for inspection tasks over the implementation of this law and Regulation No 1371/2007, and other rules laying down the rights of passengers. We also know that so far in the Republic of Croatia is fully liberalised rail freight transport, while passenger transport is liberalised only in the segment of international transport and we hope that the adoption of this law will result in new measures that will bring citizens back to trains, as it will provide them with a better quality of service and a cheaper, faster and safer journey. The liberalisation of rail passenger transport is very important, and unfortunately it is inevitable, so we need to train and increase the competitiveness of Croatian railways as soon as possible. It would point out that domestic passenger transport liberalisation is taking place in two levels, the first is to allow free access to all EU operators to perform this service and this service has been performed since 01 January 2019, and the second level of liberalisation is related to the publication of competitions for the award of public service contracts, which already exists since 24 December 2017, however, there are different exceptions such as these. contracts concluded directly, without invitation to tender, may continue until the end of the period on which they have been concluded, or for a maximum period of 10 years. In the Republic of Croatia, contracts are also concluded directly, but it is a one-year term, and I believe that we will also use this right to directly conclude a contract for a maximum of 10 years and thus enable our HZ passenger transport operator to prepare for the upcoming competition, i.e. to invest significant funds in a driving couple, thereby increasing the quality of its services. The proposed law also covers the area of protection of passenger rights in rail transport, which is a legally separated area from the rail market, but is functionally and operationally linked to the same. Strengthening the rights of passengers in rail transport is based on an existing system of international law which determines that consumer protection requirements are taken into account when determining and implementing other EU policies and activities. This proposal lays down a legal framework for the implementation of that Regulation with a particular emphasis on the determination of infringements. The regulation itself says "Because the passenger is a weaker party to the Transport Treaty, it should protect his rights in this respect." And it is precisely this bill that harmonizes passengers' rights with European standards. General rail passenger services should be of benefit to all citizens, so railway companies and station managers must ensure that stations, platformes, vehicles and other devices are accessible to persons with disabilities, and to other persons with limited mobility. And the EU's goal is to achieve a single European railway area and thus to ensure the competitiveness and sustainability of rail transport in the long term, and therefore to simplify the procedure for providing rail transport services, to raise the quality of rail transport services, and to move away from the unsustainable market business in this sector, which can be devastating, inefficient availability and public spending. In the coming period, the political and economic challenge is to dramatically increase the use of rail transport both passenger and freight, and given the EU White Paper that defines the EU transport development strategy, it is necessary to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by at least 40% in the period from 2021 to 2030, and to reduce the use of rail transport, the emissions of these greenhouse gases will be reduced. The current situation points to the need for better system management but the necessity of modernising railways, first of all, to rebuild and modernise railway infrastructure facilities. Insufficient investment in rail infrastructure and rail mobile capacity, means locomotives, compositions, wagons, back over the last twenty and more years has led to a major reduction in activity, the attractiveness of this type of transport. Here are my colleagues before me saying that these days, train and other transport transports are compared to these on TV, that they travel 24 hours to the sea from Osijek, that the passengers will board in the evening and arrive in Zagreb only in the morning. In order to change this, exits to modernising rail infrastructure and raising transport speeds. Very fast passenger trains become an increasingly attractive means of transport compared to European intercity air traffic. Europe has brought back 15 years of frameworks that strengthen the role of regulators and ensure the same legal position of transport operators on the market, in particular through the independence of the allocation of transport capacities and the fixing of fees for their use, and the EU also offers models of co-financing for the construction of new and improved existing rail infrastructures through EU funds, all due to the rise in interoperability and security at EU level. The new EU's fourth framework in preparation should increase the interest of rail transport users and make this transport branch more competitive compared to road and air traffic, a greater number of competitions should boost productivity, specialisation and innovative business models with greater use of information technologies. This should help increase passengers and burdens, as well as new rail employment and related industry, and we should prepare ourselves for this because we seem to be very late by now. Finally, it should be said that the proposed law already brings important improvements in the first reading and that the HDZ club will support it. I must also point out once again that railways as the oldest European transport system are passengers and goods, but unfortunately for several decades they have been neglected both in Croatia and the EU. Thank you very much.

The Registrar's emergency procedure combines the first and second readings. Amendments may be submitted until the end of the debate pursuant to Article 197. Rules of Procedure. Would the applicant's representative like to give an additional explanation? Mr. Mihotić, come in.
ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43330	Thank you. The Law on the Safety and Interoperability of the Railway System regulates the safety and interoperability of the railway system, as well as the measures for the development and management of safety. The basic characteristics and intensity of the applicable law are the systematic rise and improvement of the level of safety of the railway system, and the creation of a competitive rail system in accordance with EU legislation, and technical and scientific progress. In order to achieve the established legislative postulates, in particular the creation of a competitive railway system, Article 55(3) of the applicable law provides that a vehicle with its own propulsion on the railway or rail section equipped with automatic train protection devices, auto stop devices must be equipped with a compatible automatic train protection device on board, and Article 134 of the applicable law stipulates that the vehicle must be transported with those auto stop devices until 1.7.2017. The proposed amendment will extend the deadline within which railway undertakings and infrastructure managers are required to equip a vehicle with their own drive, an automatic train protection device so as to establish the installation obligation by 1 July 2018. In the light of the explicit legal provision and the non-resuming of that deadline, it would be necessary to suspend the provision of public transport services to railways, which is as such established by the Law on Railways. The provider of public transport services to railways is in a difficult financial situation and is in the process of implementing the restructuring programme. The only possible source of financing of the auto stop installation project is through the World Bank loan, whose consent was obtained on 30 May 2017. After the necessary financial resources were provided, they moved into further activities, i.e. in the implementation of the procurement procedure, and the installation of auto stop devices. In view of the circumstances laid down, the strict application of Article 134 of the Law would have inconclusive consequences on the existence of a public transport service provider of passengers to the railway company HH passenger transport. It is a matter of economic policy, given that HZ passenger transport is a significant economic operator with a large number of employees, and the issue of installing the autostop device is necessary to resolve urgently, in order to allow smooth business and public service provision. The infrastructure manager is based on the Law on Railways also performs a public service for the management of railway infrastructure, and owns a vehicle to which the deadline is previously mentioned, and the explicit application of the aforementioned legal provisions would have very similar consequences as for the public transport service providers to the railways. Railway freight carriers are in a difficult financial situation, given the prevailing impact of other modes of transport. It is important to point out that the procedures for the auto stop equipment are in place, but taking into account the time limit set and the financial sustainability of the carrier cannot be respected. In the case of freight transport, it would have a significant impact on the ability to carry out the assumed obligations of freight transport, which, for the consequence, would also have inconclusive consequences on the viability of a company engaged in freight transport and thus on the economy of the Republic of Croatia as a whole, since freight transport to the railways, although it is in a reduced range an irreplaceable form of transport in certain economic branches. The safety and interoperability law of Art. 124, paragraph 1 of point A relating to Art. In accordance with Article 12(3) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013, the railway inspectors are required to prohibit the use and punish for the infringement of the railway undertaking and infrastructure manager if the vehicles operating on the railway line do not have an installed stop device. The amendment is necessary, since it is inconceivable financial consequences for the economy of the Republic of Croatia, which could occur with the suspension of all passenger and freight, rail transport, and given the obligation to install the mentioned car stop. It is proposed that the law enters into force on the first day of its publication in the N.N. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43351	Thank you very much. Following on behalf of the HDZ club, representative Pero Ćosic. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43352	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President of the Croatian Parliament, distinguished representatives of the proposings, distinguished colleagues and colleagues. Statistics say that today, at global level, every two hours, there is a traffic accident involving a train and a motor vehicle or a natural person. And almost half of these accidents are caused by the failure of automatic signalling devices both on the train itself or on the railway track, and these shocking statistical values are unfortunately expected to take into account and look at the sample of the test or maintenance of these signal devices. Obsolete technology on trains and railways that drive daily contributes to potential catastrophic accidents, and the development of new automatic train control systems offers a solution to passenger and goods safety, but also saves the resources needed to transport them. In order to enable EU citizens, economic operators, regional and local authorities to fully enjoy the benefits arising from the established area without internal borders, it is particularly necessary to improve the interconnection and interoperability of the railway system, which will open the market and increase competitiveness. The effective interconnection between information and communication systems of different infrastructure managers and railway undertakings, i.e. the compatibility of the system, is the basis for safety but also for quality and transport costs. Unfortunately, there are still significant differences between national regulations and internal rules and technical specifications applied by railways. Since the content of the technical characteristics of individual countries, which prescribe specific dimensions, has specific characteristics. This prevents smooth rail transport across the EU network, which needs to be removed or standardised. This legislative proposal proposes extending the time limit within which railway undertakings and infrastructure managers are obliged to equip their own vehicles with their own drive, the automatic train protection device, or the auto stop device and this legal provision, as we have heard, concerns all railway system stakeholders both these HZ passenger transport countries, HZ Cargo, HZ infrastructures and all others both domestic and foreign operators that have from the Railway Safety Agency, the permit of transport on Croatian railways. In addition to the protection of victims, victims today have precise rights in the law, so it is the right to access services to support victims of violence, effective psychological and other professional assistance, protection against intimidation and retribution, protection of dignity during the examination of the victim as a witness, escorting a person from trust in taking the hearings and so on. Article 10 now defines what is considered to be domestic violence and puts in place a much wider diapason of relations so that it would be convenient or to protect victims in the family environment. There is a mandatory psychosocial treatment, then sending treatment to addiction, and in particular well-worked measures, restraining and stalking of victims and the distance of thugs from the common household. Given that this measure is issued within 24 hours, given that its duration may be from 2 months to a year, we believe that it will contribute to a better solution to this issue, that now those persons who are victims of domestic violence will have the opportunity to eventually move out will have one reasonable deadline within which they will be able to organise their life, find accommodation, possibly employment. And in some ways, we believe that this measure has contributed significantly to solving this problem that was before, which is that the victims of domestic violence were found after applying to the streets. So these are some of the more important newspapers of this law, of course they're not the only ones, but some of the better changes and the law is, for the law, we can say that it's basically good, of course it's not ideal and it can always be better and more comprehensive. Because only the Istanbul Convention will force us, means that we comprehensively regulate the legislative system and launch all institutions in the fight and protect people from domestic violence, from any form of discrimination and abuse. Namely, the Istanbul Convention, it imposes an obligation on signatories to change the position of gender role and stereotype in society. It encourages education of experts in working with victims, it raises awareness of the harmfulness of violence and the consequences of traumatic violence, it raises the quality of education in this direction, encourages cooperation between the NGOs, the media and the private sector to actively engage in the protection of women from violence and discrimination. Signatory countries must provide enough money to actively finance the treatment and assistance of victims of violence, to finance safe houses, telephone lines. It must then be fully regulated and adapted to criminal legislation in order to ensure the punishment of the heirs, in order to ensure that the procedure for the protection of the victims and the conduct of the infringement proceedings is carried out as quickly and as effectively as possible. So these are some of the measures that we will be forced to take because the current situation in our country is such that we have about 10 shelters for victims of domestic violence, that these shelters have limited capacity and that, of course, not all persons can be taken care of. Then, we have a national strategy or we don't have a National Strategy on Protection against Violence today because it's expired, it was the period until 2011 to 2016, after it expired we haven't evaluated it or brought forward a proposal for a new strategy, it's supposed to be a new strategy in the e-consultation process, I've heard it now during the debate, but I didn't know before. ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43499	Your Honor, Mr. Vice President, the Honorable Parliamentary MPs, the Honorable Parliamentarians. Here is a proposal for a law to establish that funds from a special account for the regular financing of independent representatives and members of the representative bodies of the unit of the local (regional) and the regional self-government chosen from the list of group voters and funds from a special account for the financing of the electoral promotion of candidates for independent lists or voters' list holders are excluded from the exercise, in order to enable those persons to use funds from special accounts for the purpose of which they are intended. According to the applicable law, the accounts are specific purposes for the financing of the political action of independent representatives and councilors during the year, i.e. for the financing of election promotion of candidates and independent lists, i.e. a list of voters, and the funds from those accounts must not be used for private purposes. In particular, the applicable law provides for treatment in the event of enforcement of the above-mentioned accounts for the costs incurred for private purposes. It is prescribed that independent representatives and members of the representative bodies of the unit of local and regional (regional) self-government chosen from the list of a group of voters, i.e. candidates and holders of independent lists, or holders of a list of voters' groups in the event of the implementation of funds from a special account for covering transactions made for private purposes be obliged to repay the amount of funds made by paying the executive amount to a special account within the prescribed time limit. However, as in accordance with the Law on the implementation of the budget implementation, the cash performance is carried out by all accounts in all banks according to the personal identification number of the perpetrators without its consent, such a solution in practice in the case of a number of members of the representative body of the self-government selected from the list of the voter group caused a continuing problem, in particular in the case of a high amount of enforcement. Since, after the payment of the executive amount to a special account, the same and every time is completed again and so on in continuity. This makes it impossible for council members to use funds from a special account for regular annual financing for which they are intended to prevent them from having regular political action. That solution also leads to the inability to run under equal conditions for those persons in the blockade. Namely, candidates, independent list holders, or voters' list holders, are obliged to open a special account for the financing of the electoral promotion and, at the same time, if they are in a blockade due to the performance of the accounts, in all banks according to the personal identification number of the perpetrators, the financing of the electoral promotion will be disabled. In order to address that problem, the proposed amendment to the law, instead of a solution so far, which sets out the obligation to repay executive funds from a specific account if the funds are made to cover the costs made for private purposes, proposes to establish that funds from a specific account are excluded from the application. In accordance with the proposed amendment, it is also proposed to delete the provisions which provided for a fine for a violation for independent representatives and members of the representative bodies of the self-government team selected from the list of voters, i.e. candidates and holders of lists who do not repay the amount of funds within the prescribed time limit as unnecessary. Here, thank you very much.
ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43500	Thank you, too. Before we go any further, we have two replicas. First Mr. Grbin.
ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43501	Thank you, Mr. Vice President. Today, the Committee on the Constitution discussed this bill and submitted two amendments to it. And unanimously these amendments have been submitted as unanimously to support the law. And it's about one flaw in that law that I'm going to try to sum up in this replica in a short time. In this law, funds that independent representatives, or independent councilors, etc., receive for their political work, and those obtained from the state budget are fully excluded from the exercise. However, these independent council members, MPs, etc., should spend these resources on their political work. In order to spend something earlier, they have to make contracts or other legal deals. In the event of failure to comply with their obligations and those resulting from political work, they would not be eligible for the means to do so. It is therefore proposed that such a solution as was proposed by the Government be complemented in such a way that these funds are excluded from the exercise and other than the implementation of the obligation to carry out political activity. In this way, however, it would be possible for these independent representatives, councilors, etc., who do not fulfil their obligations and are bound by their work to be forced to do so, i.e. they can be forced to collect from them. It is a solution that is in some way comparable to that of the one that follows in Article 36. The law on crafts which regulates the way in which a craftsman is responsible for the obligations arising from his activity where his personal property is excluded, we believe that such a solution would further correct the law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43524	Thank you very much. Mr. Marko Šimić Club of HZD Representatives. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43525	I thank the Vice President of the Croatian Parliament. Respected secretary of state with associates, respected colleagues and fellow MPs. The Law on the financing of political activities and electoral promotions shall regulate, among other things, the regular annual financing of political parties, independent representatives and members of the representative bodies of units of local and regional (regional) self-government elected from the list of voters, and the financing of election promotions, political parties of independent lists, i.e. the lists of a group of voters and candidates. This law was passed in 2011 and has already experienced several amendments. In this conference about 3.4 months ago, we also discussed a proposal for amending the law, which referred to the provisions of the laws governing the way in which the amount of financial resources for the work of political parties was determined and the election promotion. Today, we are discussing a part of the law here, namely provisions that require the obligation of independent representatives and members of the representative bodies of the units of local self-government elected from the list of group voters to open a special account for the regular financing of their activity, and the obligation of the holder of the list of voters and candidates to open a special account for the financing of the electoral promotion. The law also provides for fines for infringements for those entities which do not open a specific account, i.e. they do not do so by law for the prescribed and prescribed period. It should be borne in mind that this law is prescribed and that funds from those specific accounts should not be used for private purposes. In the light of the law on the implementation of the implementation of the financial assets, the implementation of the financial assets shall be carried out in all accounts, in all banks according to the OIB of the perpetrators without its consent. The law also regulates the conduct in the event of enforcement from those accounts for the costs incurred for private purposes. If we take into account that the Constitution of the Republic of Croatia has laid the basis for the consumption and respect of political freedoms in the Republic of Croatia and, if we take into account that all the costs resulting from the campaign and the political promotions in the campaign must be reported, i.e. show, and financed from a specific open account for that purpose, that solution leads to, indirectly, the inability to run under the same conditions as those in the blockade. In order to address this problem, it is necessary to exclude the performance of that specific account. A special account for the regular annual financing of independent representatives and members of representative bodies of units of local self-government selected from the list of voters and a special account for the financing of electoral promotion of independent lists, i.e. a list of groups of voters and candidates, thus enabling elected representatives as well as candidates and holders of lists to use funds from those special accounts, for which they are intended, thus enabling the smooth exercise of democratic right to political action, i.e. the exercise of the right to finance their political action. From this point on, it is acceptable for us as a Club of Representatives of the HDZ to propose that this legislative proposal, instead of a solution to which the obligation to repay the funds from a special account is established, if the funds are made to cover the costs made for private purposes, that the funds from a special account for the regular financing of independent representatives, i.e., members of representative bodies and units of local and regional (regional) self-governments selected from a list of voters, and the funds from a special account for the financing of election promotion of candidates and independent lists, i.e. holders of a list of voters are exempt from the exercise. Furthermore, these amendments also propose eradicating the provisions which provided for fines for infringements of those who do not repay the amount of funds within the prescribed time limit, which, by eradicating this earlier provision, actually loses its meaning, so we will also support this provision of the law. It is important to note that the Legislative Committee unanimously supported the adoption of this law and did not object to the proposal for an emergency procedure and to the entry into force of the first day following its publication in the NN. We will also support, of course, the amendment of the Committee on the Constitution, Rules of Procedure and the political system, which the esteemed colleague Grbin mentioned at the very beginning, and presented the position and content of the amendments. So it remains to my conclusion that the HDZ Representatives' Club will support this bill together with the amendment of the Committee on the Constitution, the Rules of Procedure and the political system. Thank you.

Article 59 The Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament have been established by the working bodies of the Croatian Parliament and delegations who have the position of the working body. The Parliamentary Assembly of the European Security and Co-operation Organisation was established by the Paris Charter for New Europe at a summit in Paris in 1990. It was organised by the final resolution of the Madrid Conference of Parliamentary Leaders of the participating countries in 1991. Some of the main tasks of the parliamentary assembly include strengthening and consolidation of the democratic institutions of the OSCE participating countries, developing institutional structures, protecting human rights, better co-operation between the existing OSCE institutions and contributing to promoting conflict resolution mechanisms. As one of the forms of international activities of the Croatian Parliament and participation in international parliamentary organisations in the Croatian Parliament, the delegation of the Croatian Parliament works as a permanent delegation at the Parliamentary Assembly of the Organisation for European Security and Cooperation. In view of the above, and given the important tasks of the OSCE Parliamentary Assembly, it is proposed that the Permanent Assembly of the Croatian Parliament at the OSCE Parliamentary Assembly has the position of the permanent working body of the Croatian Parliament. ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43578	The following point: - Report on the implementation of the National Consumer Protection Programme for the period from 2013 to 2106 is submitted by the Government of the Republic of Croatia on the basis of Article 130. Consumer protection laws. The material was delivered to the boxes. The discussion was conducted by the Committee on Economy. You received the reports at the session. Does the applicant want to give an additional explanation? Ms. Natasha Mikush, State Secretary. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43579	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President, distinguished MPs and MPs. So before you is the Report on the Implementation of the National Consumer Protection Programme for the period from 2013 to 2106. It is therefore adopted by the basis of Article 130 of the Consumer Protection Act, which is, therefore, also the Government and the Ministry of Economy as a Resolute Ministry, which is the programme itself, and is required to report to the Croatian Parliament on the implementation of measures under the consumer protection programme itself. In that period, therefore, a number of measures and activities have been implemented to raise the level of consumer protection in Croatia, which were aimed at creating a legal framework, improving market surveillance and education and informing consumers. In part of the legal framework in 2014, the Consumer Protection Act implementing the Consumer Rights Directive has been adopted, rules on distance purchase have been laid down, especially through the Internet and outside the business premises of traders, thus equating rules in all Member States and contributing to facilitating the business of traders and increasing consumer confidence. In 2016, an Alternative Dispute Resolution Act was adopted. This is particularly important for the protection of consumers in the digital environment, i.e. for those consumers who buy just online, ensure and resolve the dispute in the same way, alternative. In part of market surveillance, therefore, measures have been taken against traders who have violated consumer rights and have removed dangerous products from the market, all of which, of course, contributed to the level of protection of consumer rights and security in Croatia. Since the safety of products placed on the market for all of us as consumers is therefore of paramount importance in those cases where surveillance has been found that the products placed on the market pose a risk to the safety and health of consumers, inspectors have taken appropriate administrative measures, including the withdrawal of the same products from the market or the ban on placing them on the market. A total of 41 000 such controls were carried out during this reporting period. The report itself also includes data per year. As regards consumer education and information, it is necessary to say that one of the main backbones of consumer protection policy is to provide them with better information about their rights and the possibilities for their protection. Therefore, the Ministry of Economy has introduced and used a number of tools to raise the level of awareness and protection of consumers, i.e. the protection of their rights. Through the project Consumer consultation financed from the national budget at the annual level, four consultations, in Zagreb, Osijek, Split and Pula, were financed through the project. It has also been established through the work of the central consumer information system, allowing citizens to request, through one-stop mail, an explanation of the rules, advice or to provide instructions to the market inspection and throughout the period from 2013 to 2016, a total of 12 thousand 331 tips were provided to consumers. The Register "Don't Call", whose purpose is to protect consumers from unwanted contacts and offers via telephone or SMS messages by traders and by the end of June 2017, has been registered in the Register "Don't Call" nearly 24 thousand phone numbers. In 2013, the European Consumer Centre was established as part of the European Consumer Centre network and is helping consumers and giving advice, providing information on cross-border trade. The Ministry of Economy has been involved in strengthening consumer rights in cooperation with other state administration bodies, primarily regulatory bodies such as the Croatian Energy Regulatory Agency, the Civil Aviation Agency, the Financial Services Supervisory Agency, the Water Council, which carried out a number of activities in the course of their activities just to raise awareness and protect the economic interests of consumers. The report shall give a detailed overview of these segments of work on consumer rights protection. We believe that only implementing the measures indicates the progress of the existing consumer protection system, but it also opens up the possibility for us and ultimately gives us a commitment to further develop consumer protection policy while keeping the following key. That there is a need to further develop and improve the development of market surveillance especially in the context of digital environment and digital trade. Furthermore, to develop simple tools for consumer information and education, so that they are understandable and easily accessible to all citizens. Work further on the independence and representativeness of consumer protection associations, more to be included in the same process of unit of local and regional self-government, continue to strengthen regulators in protecting the economic interests of consumers. Here, so this is a summary of the report, of course, if there are questions, we're available for answers to the same. Thank you very much.

We changed the regulations in accordance with the needs that were so this is particularly important regarding this part of the product reduction, that it was not known how to define it. Then there are several important things that have been tied up and that have been partially neglected, but they have equally proved important. So, when Croatia joined the EU, it needed to regulate a number of things with its directives in different areas. One of the things she needed to regulate is about construction products. I'm really going to take a few sentences or it's already too late so let's try to really get past all the points of the agenda. When we entered the EU and the construction product-related directive, it is then defined in the way in which part of the EU directive is regulated, and part is left to each Member State to regulate itself dependent on the specific conditions and ways it has. And we had a few things back then. One of the things was a big public hack and there were big stories, it was tied to plastics, where at one point, plastics from one of the EU members came to our market. But since Croatia is partly a continental country, and in part the Mediterranean country did not respond unfortunately either to the Mediterranean area or to the continental area. In the area of continental snowfall, they were not allowed at all for conditions in snow, and in the Mediterranean part the wind blew. So all of a sudden, something that came into our market absolutely when it was significantly cheaper than our products didn't fit the market. And thanks to the work of the inspection and everything else, we managed to regulate that part. Equally, it is a condition that must satisfy all other regulations and obtain all other special conditions. In particular, when it comes to base stations, so these are the objects being set, they must be planned by a document of spatial arrangement. Their location must be defined, and when it comes to antennae connections on buildings, which cause the problem related to radiation and everything else, they must be planned by documents of spatial arrangement that are valid in a given space, whether it is the spatial plan of the municipality or the city or... Therefore, the fact that something is defined by the Simple Works Regulation does not mean that it is not planned by documents of spatial arrangement. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] If someone put it past that, then they're setting it up and they're gonna have to move. Similarly, what you need to know is that the whole EU regulation, which we are trying to achieve, and we are still much stricter than the rest of the EU countries, has much more work, which can be done without local and construction permits. So in particular, when it comes to the balcony, if, for example, the balcony is closed today, and it's not for a change, the purposes of giving the balcony, or the log, remain log, but it's closed, it doesn't need permission, some things just need a useful permit to regulate it properly. So that regulation adopted by the Law on Spatial Planning and Construction made sure, in accordance with other EU regulations, that we somehow loosen procedures for which no local, or building permit is required.

It is good to point out, and it also says in the report that the National Consumer Protection Programme is a strategic document of the Republic of Croatia in a consumer protection policy that sets out objectives, measures, priority areas, and activities in implementing consumer protection policy for a period of four years. It is also good to recall that the National Consumer Protection Programme for the years mentioned was adopted in July 2013 in this House. The Ministry of Economy, entrepreneurship and crafts of the then Ministry of Economy as a resolute body for consumer protection policy in the Republic of Croatia carried out activities related to the implementation of consumer protection policy and the coordination of the work of all consumer safety holders in relation to the implementation of the national programme. The main objective of consumer protection policy in the Republic of Croatia is to improve the quality of life of citizens and is part of the EU's strategic objective of ensuring that all EU citizens enjoy high standards of consumer protection as well as Croatia. The aim of the analysis of the implementation of the national programme is to identify the real situation in the area of consumer protection or to assess whether the consumer protection system has been improved compared to the previous period. Although the process of building a Croatian consumer protection system has not yet been achieved since the four years mentioned in the Republic of Croatia in this area has not yet been completed. Only the report is divided into several chapters, some of which are the regulatory framework, the institutional framework, the supervisory market. It should be pointed out that the implementation of the National Consumer Protection Programme resulted in awareness raising of their rights, which is the relative objective of this programme. In order to achieve that consumer protection, it is adapted to European rules, consumer advice is organised, instruments are set up for easier dispute resolution, the right to return to an invalid product, high safety standards as well as consumer information rights on product content and quality, etc. The expansion of Internet trade in the common market has affected the need to create a single regulatory framework for all EU members that provides legal certainty for consumers and traders, thus contributing to the creation of an incentive environment for cross-border trade, reducing business costs and higher additional values in the economy. The aim of the harmonisation of consumer protection rules was to remove barriers to the free movement of goods and services in the common market arising from different rules in the Member States, thus restoring the confidence of consumers and traders in the functioning of the common market and thereby taking advantage of it. Equal operating rules at EU level on the one hand would contribute to reducing business costs and opening up space for the development and growth of economic activities, small and medium-sized enterprises, which would provide the opportunity to seek business opportunities in other Member States, and on the other hand would restore consumer confidence and encourage the use of all the advantages of the common market. The result was the adoption of a Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on consumer rights as early as 2011. a time limit has been set within which the consumer may change his/her mind regarding the conclusion of a distance sale contract or the sale of extra-business premises for 14 days as well as a one-sided termination of the contract in relation to the return of goods and money, and the obligations of traders and consumers and cases where the consumer changes his/her mind regarding the conclusion of a distance or sale contract outside the business premises, and even in cases where the consumer decides to unilaterally terminate the contract after the goods are used to a greater extent than is necessary to determine its characteristics and functioning. This is regulated by the Consumer Protection Act, which has, in addition to the above mentioned, improved some of the provisions which have been shown by practice that a great violation of consumer rights refers to provisions on action sales, reduction and sale. We know that a large number of our consumers are currently buying during the seasonal downsizing of stocks and sales, and unfortunately some trade chains have been exploiting it to the detriment of end-users. Therefore, it was necessary to edit this area more closely. The report also shows the need to strengthen the role of local self-government units in implementing consumer protection policies, in particular in the consultation section. It is therefore prescribed that all units of local self-government in their areas are obliged to conduct education and to bring as much knowledge as possible to their consumers. The harmonisation of the starting date of the downturn season with the business bang of traders as well as certain types of goods is governed by the Regulation on the conditions and manner of implementation of the December 2015 seasonal reductions. We know that then deadlines are set so that more or less everyone knows that this deadline lasts no more than 60 days including December and summer time. Furthermore, over the last decades, the European Commission has implemented a comprehensive development of legal gains in the field of consumer protection, including the revision of the Travel Packages Directive, the holiday packages, the packages resulting from the adoption of a new Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council. As tourism is one of the largest branches of the economy for the Republic of Croatia and taking into account the immeasurable importance of adequate regulation and equalisation of the rights of passengers and obligations of travel organisers when buying a package of arrangements within the single European market, the Republic of Croatia has been very actively involved in the revision of the directive in question, taking into account the interests of passengers and consumers and travel organisers in order to reconcile the interests of these entities in the most appropriate way. Furthermore, in October 2014 the Internal Design Regulation of the Ministry of Economy within the Department of Trade and the Internal Market, the goods, services and consumer protection sector was established a consumer protection policy service that proposes and implements the policy of all consumers, proposes measures to improve the efficiency of the overall consumer protection system in the area of the Republic of Croatia and implements whether everything with the right of EU acquis is in the field of consumer protection, monitors the achievement of the objectives and tasks set out in the policy strategy, consumer protection, etc. The ministry therefore focused its activities on continuous education and information to the public on all relevant consumer issues in order to facilitate consumer compliance with many complex sectoral rules and to enable more effective exercise of their rights. It should welcome the continued development of a central consumer information system that is possible for all in one place to request explanations of regulations and procedures, advice or to provide instructions for the application for inspection supervision related to the implementation of their consumer rights. Furthermore, the report reads that not many consumer protection associations have been forgotten, which have been granted grants to advise, inform and educate consumers themselves. It is well noted that the inclusion of local, local and regional self-government units in these processes has been of paramount importance since there has been no adequate development of local policy and consumer protection in the past period. The 2014 Consumer Protection Act strengthened the role and gave emphasis to the local community to coordinate and coordinate it. It should certainly be recalled that the National Consumer Protection Council is an advisory body of the Government of the Republic of Croatia and one of the most important policy holders of consumer protection. The Croatian Chamber of Commerce and Croatian Chamber of Commerce, Croatian Employers' Association, Commercial Court, Consumer Protection Associations, Local Self-Government Units, etc. As part of the National Council, four committees chaired by representatives of the regulatory bodies in the Council, such as electronic communications for financial services, energy, water services, etc., have been established. Furthermore, what is important is the National Consumer Protection Programme for the period of the mentioned years of market surveillance, the area of consumer protection for the reporting period was planned in the State Inspectorate, and we heard that the State Inspectorate of the Law on Amendments and Supplements of January 2014 on the establishment and part-round of ministries and other central bodies, so he stopped working with the work of the State Inspectorate and part of the affairs related to inspection tasks, consumer protection became the responsibility of the ministry. In order not to conclude, the implementation of the National Consumer Protection Programme for 2013-2016 created the conditions for Croatia's citizens as consumers to make economically justified and informed decisions. This report indicates that all stakeholders were actively involved in achieving the basic objective, which is to raise consumer awareness of their rights, adapting to European consumer protection rules. The Republic of Croatia has removed market barriers and integrated the national market into the common market with products and services of equal standards of safety and quality as well as with equal standards of consumer protection. It sought to make the consumer a centre of interest for entrepreneurs. Today, special attention is paid to consumers in the Republic of Croatia and increased attention. Consumer consultation has been organised, already mentioned here by the secretary of state at national level, and improved instruments have been established to facilitate the resolution of complaints such as a single consumer phone and a central consumer information system. Measures have also been taken to ensure the consumer’s freedom to choose products and services, the right to return an invalid product, high safety and standards, as well as the right to information on product content and quality, the right to clear and transparent contractual provisions and to protect against misleading and aggressive business practices. Measures have also been taken to ensure the protection of consumer rights on travel in the country and abroad, as well as measures to prevent and violate consumer rights in cross-border trade and to achieve greater security rights. It is good to recall that a European Consumer Centre was established within the Ministry in 2013 to address consumer complaints during cross-border purchases. Consumer and trader education has sensitized the public about the importance and need of consumer protection and with a view to raising the overall level of awareness. The implementation of the National Consumer Protection Programme for 2013-2016 points to significant progress in the existing system of protection and opens the possibility of developing policies and protecting consumers in areas where certain shifts are to be made. ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43615	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President, ladies and gentlemen of the Croatian Parliament. In front of you is the Annual Defence Report for 2016, which describes to you and the Croatian public how the defence portfolio works, which it develops, how it fulfils its social role and has the means to which it is entrusted. In the strategic context of 2016, the trend of the growing instability of the political security environment in Europe and the world continued. The nature of security challenges and threats is becoming more complex and unpredictable and the interdependence of external and internal security is evident. Recognising these trends, the Croatian government launched a process of adopting a new National Security Strategy and drafting the Law on the Homeland Security System and directed them to the Croatian Parliament a few days ago for adoption. For the period 2016-2016, the The EU has made a significant step towards institutional improvement of the common security and defence policy through the presentation of a global strategy for foreign and security policy as well as plans for its implementation. In 2016, priorities in the design and implementation of defence policy at the Ministry of Defence concerned the continuation of the construction of defence capabilities identified by the long-term development plan of the Armed Forces and NATO with the objectives of capacity. As a responsible member of the International Community, the Republic of Croatia continued to contribute to the stabilisation of crisis hotspots and the expansion of the area of peace and security. In line with such commitment, the Armed Forces continued to participate in missions and peace support operations and thus continued our contribution to building international security. The most important security in 2016 in the context of NATO was the summit held in early July in Warsaw. In response to the amended geostrategic environment, the summit was attended by a decision on the Allied Dissuasion and Defence Act on the sending of forces to the northeast of the alliance in the context of a strengthened frontal presence. During 2016, activities were carried out aimed at achieving the projected personal structure of the Armed Forces, as well as improving the quality of staff through the balancing and coordination of primary development of support and personnel. A negative trend in the collapse of the defence budget has been halted, with around 25% of the budget being lost in the last 8 years. The growth of the defence budget in 2017 is 400 million kuna compared to 2016. In 2017, the ficus is on a man in a defence vault. In accordance with the plan for the admission of active military personnel, 800 soldiers were admitted in 2016, 15 candidates for officers of which 9 doctors of medicine, and on honorary duties in the active military service indefinitely, 250 contracted soldiers were accepted. In December 2016 a new Internal Design Regulation of the Ministry of Defence was adopted and new solutions were adopted on the schedule for all employees of the Ministry of Defence. For the reception of new officials and rejuvenation of the system, public officials will be invited to tender for the reception of that category of staff within a few days. At the end of 2016, pre-conditions were created and since 01 January 2017 improvements were made to the status of active military personnel by amending several regulations concerning the assessment of active military personnel, the establishment of the rights of cadets to collective insurance, a rule relating to military uniforms, a rule relating to reception conditions for active military personnel in such a way that under equal conditions the child has not only the mortally injured and missing Croatian defender from the Homeland War but also the child of every Croatian defender from the Homeland War. Equivalence of subsidies for the diet of officers and officers, increase of subsidies for soldiers who remain in the military, increase the monthly fee to volunteers from 1190 to 2000 kuna, and a rule on military specialists has been adopted. During 2016, as a prerequisite for the long-term development of the defence portfolio, the emphasis is on continuing the transformation of the Croatian Military University, Dr. Franjo Tudjman in a higher education and scientific research facility with the integration of the University Community of the Republic of Croatia. Equipment and modernisation in accordance with the means available have been implemented with the aim of achieving the capacity to carry out the missions and tasks of the Armed Forces and to achieve these objectives. The implementation of the equipment and modernisation programme for all branches of the Armed Forces was focused on redefined priorities. The involvement of the manufacturer of military equipment from the Republic of Croatia and the promotion of growth of the Croatian economy with respect to competition has been retained. By the end of 2016, under the current long-term development plan of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Croatia, a decision was to be taken on the possibilities and modes of the development of the capacity to protect airspace. Therefore, an expert team was established on the multi-purpose combat aircraft project to carry out the multi-purpose combat aircraft procurement operations. The tasks of the expert team are to produce a tactical and technical study by 30 June 2017, to prepare elements for the making of bid applications, to make a feasibility study based on the collected offers from potential bidders through cost-benefit analysis to prepare for further decisions. The activities of equipment and modernization of Croatian Air Force and anti-aircraft defenses were aimed at equipping the reconnaissance helicopters of Kiowa Warrior. During 2016 activities focused on continuing the equipment of 126 base armored vehicles Patria sub-systems of arms and special equipment. The implementation of the 2000 Haubice vest project continued in April 2017, the first two solo hoodies of a total of 16 were brought to the Republic of Croatia. In accordance with the dynamics of delivery of other hoodies, it was planned by the end of 2017. The main project of equipment and modernisation of the Croatian Navy in 2016 was to continue the construction of coastal patrol ships. Out of the most important infrastructure projects, we highlight the military storage complex Doljani, the Vojarna in Sinj to ensure the temporary accommodation of the engineering unit, the construction of a command facility in a military facility in Petrinja, the construction of a military facility in Vukovar, the construction of a located building in the military Savudrija, the reconstruction of the kitchen in military Croatia Zagreb, the construction of a data centre within the project of network computer systems and accommodation facilities within the Croatian Military University. Franjo Tudjman. In 2016, armed forces carried out activities in the full implementation of missions and tasks. In the peacetime conditions of the activities in 2016, they focused on the surveillance of airspace and maritime space and the contribution to the Allied collective defence. A total of 55 exercises, 17 domestic and 38 international exercises took place in 2016, increasing participation in international exercises. The implementation of the task of supporting civil institutions has proved the readiness of the Armed Forces as an inseparable part of the identity of the state and society. This was evident during the aid of flooded areas, fire extinguishing, in the rescue of emergency medical transport to the Republic of Croatia, and we highlight our participation in providing humanitarian assistance in fire-fighting tasks in the state of Israel in November last year. Dear MPs and MPs, I suggest you adopt the Annual Defence Report 2016. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43622	Finally, we have the last item of daily work: - Proposal for a Strategy and Action Plan for the protection of the nature of the Republic of Croatia for the period from 2017 to 2025. The applicant shall be the Government of the Republic of Croatia pursuant to Article 11. The Nature Protection Act. You've received the material into the boxes. ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43623	Honorable parliamentary deputies and MPs. Before you is the Strategy and Action Plan for the Protection of Nature of the Republic of Croatia. It is a roof document that ensures long-term protection of all the ingredients of biodiversity and provides a framework for the management of nature protection until 2025. Let me remind you that preserved nature represents the highest value of the constitutional order of the Republic of Croatia. The rich and diverse nature is one of the most valuable resources we have, it is the source of life and the foundation of economic development. International initiatives dealing with the economy of the ecosystem have become aware of the high value of ecosystem services that must be identified and evaluated in Croatia in the coming period. The Natura 2000 ecological network, among other things, brings significant economic benefits to EU Member States. Only the value of a recreational visit to these areas amounts to 5 to 9 billion euros per year. At European level, the value of the Natura 2000 ecological network area is recognised in particular as investment in its conservation is considered desirable because it directly contributes to the Europe 2020 objectives for growth and employment, given that Natura 2000 may be the direct drivers of the development of local, regional communities and their economies. In view of the obligations arising from the global strategic biodiversity plan 2011 to 2020, the Republic of Croatia had to develop a new Strategy and Action Plan for Nature Protection for the period 2017 to 2025. The obligation to draw up a strategy is also prescribed by the Nature Protection Act. The strategy sets out long-term objectives and guidelines for the preservation of biodiversity and geo alleviation, as well as how to implement and defines a total of 5 strategic objectives. The first strategic objective of this Strategy concerns the need to increase the effectiveness of basic nature protection mechanisms, the second strategic objective creates a framework for reducing direct pressure on nature and promoting sustainable use of natural resources. The third strategic objective is to strengthen the capacity of the nature protection system while the 4th objective provides a framework for increasing knowledge and availability of data on nature. The 5th and last strategic objective is to raise the level of knowledge, understanding and public support for nature protection. Starting with the 5 strategic objectives set out above, the Strategy defines 20 specific objectives and 107 activities. I would point out that the Strategy does not introduce new mechanisms that would additionally burden the state budget, the county budget, cities or municipalities and extra budget users. This strategy also has a positive impact on the preparation for the use of EU funds, given that the objectives and activities of the Strategy contribute to the achievement of the objectives of the Competitiveness and Cohesion Operational Programme for the period 2014-2020. The implementation of the strategy-planned activities will contribute to better absorption of EU funds. Finally, I would like to point out that by supporting this document, we are ensuring the long-term conservation of nature as a fundamental value and potential for further development of the Republic of Croatia and its affirmation in the European environment as a country with developed awareness of the importance of the conservation of natural wealth that provides multiple developmental directions for smart, thinking and sustainable use. I thank you for your direction in the hope that the Croatian Parliament will support the adoption of this basic strategic document for the protection of the nature of the Republic of Croatia for a period of time. Thank you very much.
