ParlaMint-HR_2017-07-05-0.u43643	Thank you very much, Vice President, Your Honor, Secretary of State. So I think we can all agree here that the preservation of nature and human environment represents the highest values of the constitutional order of the Republic of Croatia. This strategy is, in fact, a fundamental nature protection document that seeks to define long-term objectives and guidelines for the preservation of biodiversity and geo-proliferation. The strategy has defined vision, strategic and specific objectives and a series of activities that follow from them. It is precisely intersectoral cooperation and understanding between individual sectors that is necessary for the survival and progress of our society and for nature conservation. On this occasion, I would like to emphasise the importance and role of the forestry sector and the forest industry in implementing this strategy and action plan, as we often forget and ignore this role very often. The fundamental principles of Croatian forestry are sustainable management with the preservation of the natural structure, the diversity of forests, and the permanent increase in stability and quality of economic and general beneficial forest functions. The Convention on Biodiversity is a globally adopted basic document for the protection of biodiversity. It was adopted in Rio in 1992 and the Republic of Croatia became a party to this convention by adopting legislation on the adoption of it. The EU's biodiversity strategy was also adopted on the trail of the 2011 convention. The forests of the Republic of Croatia are internationally known as an exceptional source of biodiversity. It is important to note that even with all the natural advantages that we have in our forests, that is, forest eco systems would not be in the state in which they are today if there were no forest professions and sciences that govern forests on the principles of nature and perseverance. The value and role of forests or forest land in nature protection is sufficient and the fact that almost half of the land area of the Republic of Croatia is under forests, of which 97% of these forests are wasteland. This strategy aims to preserve the diversity of living nature and restore it through five strategic objectives. And like I said, I'm going to try briefly to look back from the point of view of forestry and forestry on each one of them. The first strategic objective involves increasing the effectiveness of basic nature protection mechanisms. The accession of the EU Republic of Croatia also identified the areas of the NATURA 2000 ecological network and of the total area of the land area of the Republic of Croatia, which is located under that network of more than 50%, is located in the forest-economic area. In addition, when designing the programme and the basis for forest management, the competent ministry shall issue conditions of nature protection for each economic unit, and shall require the State Nature Protection Office to draw up expert bases for each economic unit. These documents contain descriptions of the area of the ecological network, potential areas of the ecological network. There are also guidelines inside, and protection measures required to be installed in forest management programmes. By reducing direct pressure on nature and promoting sustainable management of natural resources, it is defined as the second strategic objective of this strategy and plan. Organized forestry in Croatia at least in this form as we know it dates back to the mid-18th century. At that time, the first administration of the Karlovo Generalate forest was established and several laws were passed, i.e. several so-called so-called "Karlova Generalate". The forest order of which is best known is that of Mary Teresa's 1769 forest order. It was actually the first one we can say the forest law in which Croatian forestry is introduced for the first time the concept of sustainable or sustainable management. A notion that became known globally only at the end of the 1980s. During its 250-year practice, our forestry did not allow for the removal of natural forests and habitat degradation either by clean seeds and by raising artificial forests, which is unfortunately done by most Western European countries. Forest management is also planned and prescribed by forest-economic plans and programmes and is of breeding character. It advocates the management of indigenous species which is in the spirit of this strategy and this plan, and with natural rejuvenation. It should be noted that, in fact, almost 80% of the total forest area is managed according to FSC principles which confirm compliance with all the principles of secret, or sustainable management. Long-term planning, regular revision and restoration of forest-economic plans ensure forest management within the framework of growth and within the framework of the rules of the profession. The third strategic objective is defined as strengthening the capacity of the nature protection system. It is nature protection that depends on the strength of the system it implements, as well as on the education of those who do so and who participate in this system. The protection of forests and forest eco-systems is defined by the Law on Forests and the Regulation on forest conservation and is an integral part of the forest management process. It is prescribed that the conservation of forests may be carried out exclusively by a person with a minimum average level of expertise, subject to other conditions prescribed by specific regulations. This ensures the minimum level of education as well as in one hand the lifelong improvement that is further defined by certain regulations. Most of the tasks of these forest guards, i.e. logars as we call them, are directly related to the system of nature protection in the spirit of this strategy and in the spirit of this plan. The fourth strategic objective is to mention increasing knowledge and availability of data on nature. It is noted that the lack of human potential for data collection and processing and the lack of financial resources is precisely the biggest challenge in the implementation of this objective. That is why it is necessary to insist on strengthening all those systems that actually work through their day - to - day activities. Eco-systems, i.e. the wealth they produce, that is, all their benefits are called eco-system services and so far the strategy has not recognized them and has not dealt directly with them. But this topic was recognized in the 1990s through the system by the general user of the forest functions. For those services, all legal, natural persons carrying out economic activities paid a fee or still paid, which unfortunately over the past years has been reduced several times and today amounts to 0.0265% of total income. Unfortunately, complaints and callings are actually due to this fee, but only when there is a disturbance of the common useful functions of the forest whether it is due to fires, floods, certain natural... or we can see their role and significance, even though we often take them for granted. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] I'll mention that only soil protection from erosion, the prevention of floods, the purification of water, the air, the mitigation of greenhouse effects and the like. And the last goal of this strategy is to raise the level of knowledge, understanding and public support for nature protection. Information, education of the public is a very important factor in the system of nature protection and precisely the topic of nature protection we build and is woven through education from the earliest stages of education, and beyond. However, the most powerful touch and public impact have institutions that can bring nature protection values closer to and specifically on the ground through teaching paths, panels, installations in nature and the like. Many of them are located in the areas of forest eco-systems and, along with public institutions, a great contribution to this goal is given by the company of the Croatian Forests, as well as the Croatian Forest Society. And finally, to conclude, because my time is slowly leaking. Forests and forest land account for 47% of the total land area of the Republic of Croatia. Forest eco systems are highly complex and, fortunately, most of our eco systems can be, for example, for many in the world, through the sphere of management, either through the sphere of forest protection, or through the protection of nature indirectly. Similarly, it is necessary to highlight the need especially for this topic of listening to professions and institutions, and to implement the instructions they give to avoid further anomalies, further irregularities in protecting forests which we have had the opportunity to see in the problems of the underpasses in the Gorski Kotar which was created after the catastrophic icy.
